a:["$","p",null,{"children":"An independent set is a subset of nodes in a graph such that no two selected nodes share an edge. A maximum independent set (MIS) is an independent set with the largest possible number of nodes. Applications of MIS include scheduling, resource allocation, and network design.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]
b:["$","p",null,{"children":"MIS is NP-hard. In an exhaustive search, every additional node doubles the number of subsets to consider, so this approach is impractical even for moderately sized graphs. QOBLIB includes MIS as one of its ten problem classes, so it is a useful benchmark for evaluating quantum optimization workflows on real hardware.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

17:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"It can tackle problems with more binary variables than the QPU has qubits."}]," Presolve applies reduction rules that can fix variables and simplify the remaining QUBO while preserving at least one optimal solution. Empirically, presolve can also make the remaining QUBO sparser, which helps the QRAC-based encoding that follows it."],"className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":["The solver then uses a QRAC-based encoding derived from Quantum Random Access Optimization (QRAO) ",["$","$L2",null,{"href":"#references","children":"[3]"}]," to represent multiple variables (up to three) with no direct QUBO interaction on the same qubit."],"className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":"The reductions from presolve and encoding depend on the problem structure.
